## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #164: the job's error counts DNS replies refused for size or record count (PR #168)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `33ea51b`, at head `ae46dda`; branch deleted, #164 closed.
- **Done:** [PR #168](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/168) closes #164.
  - `probe.py`: when no server gave a usable answer and one attempt ended in `ReplyTooLarge` or `ReplyTooManyRecords`, `ptr` returns `UnreadReplies(too_large, too_many_records)` instead of None. Only the query's own server can cause it.
  - `scan.py`:
    - `refused_unread`: the refusal sentence.
    - `no_answer`: the unanswered count, then the refusals.
    - `cut_short`: the refusals after a PTR timeout.
    - `error_text`: keeps the counts whole or drops them whole.
  - `protocol.py`: `scan_error_fits`.
  - Spec §5.2 says what the owner is told and what to do: `minimal-responses` in BIND or Unbound; for a forwarder (dnsmasq, Pi-hole, AdGuard Home, Technitium, a router), the upstream that produces the PTR answer. A PTR record set that is itself over the limit is the zone's own data.
  - Brief §4.6 and the `AGENTS.md` bullet "The agent never executes…" each get one sentence. The merge with #167 kept `main`'s wording and added only that sentence.
- **The error now:**
  - With nothing refused, the text is unchanged from before #164.
  - With refusals: `rdns: no DNS server gave a usable answer for 3 of 4 addresses, names kept; the agent refused a reply over 1232 bytes in 2 lookups, over 1 question or 32 records in 1 lookup`.
  - A limit that refused nothing is left out, and one lookup reads "1 lookup".
  - After a deadline: `timed out after T s during the PTR lookups; rdns: before the timeout, the agent refused a reply …`, counted over the lookups that returned. The plain unanswered count stays out, as before.
- **Longest error:** 495 characters against the 500 cap, a margin of 5; 405 in the timeout form. Past the cap the counts are dropped whole, never cut before a number.
- **Decisions:** PR Deliberate calls 1–6.
  - The count travels as a third "no usable answer" value; None stays legal and no fake resolver had to change.
  - A lookup a later server answered is not counted.
  - A lookup can be in both counts.
  - The second count covers both header limits.
  - "Their stored names are kept" becomes "names kept" when refusals are reported, so the sentence fits.
- **Review:**
  - Codex and Greptile were clean on `30246ab`; Codex was clean on `ae46dda`, which Greptile did not re-review.
  - Briefed round 1 (Codex, GPT-6 Sol, at `30246ab`): GO with four P3s, all fixed in `c197f10`:
    1. a deadline hid known refusals;
    2. the cap was held only by a 2-character margin;
    3. the wording ("for 0", no unit, "or 1 question");
    4. two coverage gaps (malformed-only rows, unequal counts).
  - The orchestrator read the fixes against the remedies; no round 2.
  - Twelve mutations all fail tests, the reviewer's nine included.
- **Final counts at `ae46dda`:** agent 1,783 passed, 4 skipped (`main` before it: 1,741 passed, 4 skipped; 42 new); server 1,357 passed; web `install.test.js` 18 passed.
- **State:** merged. No schema change; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` stays 2; no wire change; `test_invariants.py` unchanged.
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR.
- **Untested or assumed:**
  - No real resolver that sends a non-minimal reply over 32 records, or ignores the advertised payload; loopback responders stand in for both.
  - The forwarder guidance comes from the products' documentation, not from running them.
  - The longest-error test lists the failure texts `probe.py` raises today. A new raise site, or an exception name longer than the builtins' 22 characters, could drop the counts but never cut them.
  - How the UI shows a long `last_scan_error`.
- **Next:** #170 (a first server whose replies are refused while a later one answers goes unreported). Then #93 or #20, one at a time; all touch `probe.py`.

## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #159: publish-release stops when it cannot tell whether the tag has a release (PR #166)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `bc3ba04`, at head `5efdd34`; branch deleted, #159 closed. Tests: 1,723 agent at the head; 1,741 passed and 4 skipped on the tree merged with `main` after PR #167 (orchestrator's run).
- **Done:** [PR #166](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/166).
  - `release.yml`, `publish-release`: it reads the repository's releases page by page (`gh api …/releases?per_page=100&page=N`, no `--paginate`). The jq prints `page <length>` first for a JSON array, then `<id> <draft>` for each release whose tag is the tag (`env.TAG`). Each answer must print its marker. The list ends at the first page shorter than 100, and an attempt that reaches page 20 full fails.
    - No release → `gh release create`, as before.
    - One release → the wheel is checked or attached, and a draft is published, as before.
    - More than one → stop with nothing changed (gh's `FetchRelease` would answer with either).
    - Could not tell → three attempts (15/30/45 s), each from page 1 with the list emptied, then stop with nothing created, uploaded or edited.
    - gh's error text goes on one warning line.
    - `publish-image`, the names and the permissions are unchanged.
  - `release_stubs.py`: the `gh release view` model and `lookup_wins` are gone. A one-page `gh api` stand-in, from gh 2.105 `pkg/cmd/api` and go-gh 2.17 jq, refuses any other argument list. `FetchRelease` calls are refused when a published release and a draft share the tag.
  - `test_release.py`: `test_only_the_two_publish_steps_are_given_the_token`. A credential is the `secrets` context, or the `github` context reached any way but one named property other than `token` (`toJSON(github)` included). 21 placements are refused and 8 named-property expressions allowed.
  - Docs: `.agents/releases.md` (the jobs table, and what the owner sees and does for each stop); the `AGENTS.md` "One version" bullet; one sentence each in spec §5.6 and brief §2.4.
- **Review:**
  - Briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) at `640b30d`: GO with two P3s, the whole `github` context and a later-page `204`. Both were fixed in `5efdd34` and read by the orchestrator against the remedies (a page with no marker taken for the end of the list failed five tests).
  - Automatic reviews: Codex clean at `640b30d` and `5efdd34`; Greptile no findings at `640b30d`, not retriggered.
- **Decisions:** PR Deliberate calls 1–6:
  - the list endpoint rather than get-by-tag, whose 404 is not proof of absence;
  - more than one release stops the job;
  - a marker on every page, asked for page by page, at most 20 pages;
  - the retry copied from `publish-image`;
  - the scope of the token rule;
  - the tripwire changed only in the stub's accepted call, with no new shell word.
- **State:** merged.
  - Mutations caught at `5efdd34` (bash 5, publish tests, the author's): old behaviour 38 failed, no retry 41, `TAG=` dropped 25, more-than-one branch 8, a page without its marker ending the list 6, page 1 as the whole list 16, no page bound 2, list not emptied 1, newlines kept 1.
  - Publish tests pass on bash 3.2 (macOS) and 5.2.37 (`--network none`).
  - No schema change, no agent code change.
- **Untested or assumed (nothing has ever been published by this workflow):**
  - that the job's `contents: write` token counts as "push access", so drafts appear in the list;
  - that a draft's `tag_name` in the list is the tag it will publish under;
  - that GitHub fills every page but the last and leaves no release out;
  - gh's real output for each answer (the stand-in is read from source, not run against gh);
  - the 20-page stop, which no real repository has reached;
  - that a re-run attempt can download the artefact (still `.agents/releases.md` step 5).
- **Waiting on the owner:** the first tag, and the release checklist with it.
- **Next:**
  - On the first release, check that `publish-release` lists the tag's draft or release as expected (its log shows each page read).
  - Optional follow-up, not filed: `publish-image`'s warning prints skopeo's stderr with its newlines.

## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #136: the CA bundle's access asked of the kernel, as the service account (PR #167)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `069af99`, at head `6429037`; branch deleted, #136 closed. Agent suite at the head, merged with `main` at `3feec43`: 1,662 passed, 4 skipped (the root-only cases). As root on Linux, `test_install.py` + `test_invariants.py`: 595 passed, 2 skipped. Server 1,357, web 188.
- **Done:** [PR #167](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/167) (`136-ca-access-as-service`).
  - `install.read_as` replaces `blocked_for`/`_allowed`. It runs `head -c 1 -- <path>` as the service user, and `test -e <path>` only when that fails, to tell "the file itself" from "a directory or link on the way".
  - The programs run through `subprocess.run(user=, group=, extra_groups=)`, with `COMMAND_ENV`, `cwd="/"`, stdio on `/dev/null`, no shell and `CA_CHECK_TIMEOUT_S` (10 s).
  - `Host.service_reads` passes `os.getgrouplist(SERVICE_USER, gid)` and prints the command without the path (#140).
  - `NOT_CHECKED` (a program missing or refused, or past the deadline) refuses the install before the exchange. `install.py` no longer imports `trust`.
  - Tripwire: `test_invariants.PROCESSES_STARTED_IN` (`Host.run`, `read_as.as_user`, with the exact keywords) and `SUBPROCESS_NAMES`.
    - `_process_starts` refuses the module named anywhere but as an attribute base (an import or assignment alias, a default argument), the string `'subprocess'`, `from subprocess import`, other names, an uncalled `run`, an `env` other than `COMMAND_ENV`, and `**keywords`.
    - No forbidden-name list changed.
  - `AGENTS.md` ("Enrolment tokens" and "agent never executes" bullets) and spec §5.6 updated. The brief has no CA-check text.
- **Verified:** as root in a `--network none` Debian 13 / systemd 257 container, with the server on loopback over HTTPS and agent wheels built from the branch and from `main`, by the author and again by the reviewer.
  - ACL that denies the account: the branch refuses with the token unused, and the same token enrols once the entry is gone. `main` spent the token, and the unit logged `CERTIFICATE_VERIFY_FAILED`.
  - Supplementary group: the branch enrols and checks in; `main` refused.
  - No image had `setfacl` and there was no network to add it, so the ACL was written as the `system.posix_acl_access` xattr (`deny_by_acl` in the tests).
- **Decisions:** PR Deliberate calls 1–9.
  - Fixed programs through `subprocess`'s user switching, not `os.fork` (which would copy root's memory, the enrolment token included, into the account's process), not Python run as the account, not `access(2)`.
  - The operator's `--ca-file` path is the one argument that is not fixed.
  - A check that cannot be made refuses the install.
  - `unreachable()` (the venv check, others' bits) is left as it was: a possible follow-up.
- **Review:** briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) GO at `2db1f1c` with two P3s, both fixed in `6429037` and answered on the PR:
  - an assignment alias of `subprocess` passed the tripwire; the reproduction now fails 13 tests;
  - with the deadline removed, the FIFO test hung the suite; it now fails at 5 s on its own bound (`FIFO_BOUND_S`), kills the process groups and reaps them, and no `head` is left.

  Automatic reviews: Greptile, one P2, fixed in `2db1f1c` (the root fixture no longer changes the mode of `--basetemp`'s parent directories); Codex clean on `a110963`, `2db1f1c` and `6429037`.
- **State:** merged. No schema change, no protocol change, no new import.
- **Untested / assumed:**
  - Ptrace (recorded, not rated): after exec, a process already running as a reused account can ptrace `head` or `test` where `ptrace_scope=0` and falsify the answer. Such an account can already sabotage its own agent, so the mechanism stands.
  - The deadline bounds only an interruptible wait; only the FIFO case was measured. A program in an uninterruptible wait (a hard network mount) is not reaped promptly, and `_ca_file`'s stat, the group lookup and starting the process are outside the deadline.
  - The check answers for the unit `install` writes, not one a drop-in changes (`SupplementaryGroups=`, `InaccessiblePaths=`, a private mount namespace, the unit's own security profile).
  - Also untested: AppArmor or SELinux, distributions other than Debian 13, busybox built without `head -c`, an unprivileged LXC.
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR.
- **Next:** #165 (refuse an agent the server cannot answer before its token is spent) is not started.

## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #94: pin the agent's base image by digest (PR #161)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `c652f2d`, at head `7d298f1`; branch deleted. Entry committed by the orchestrator, with the state lines brought up to date.
- **Done:** [PR #161](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/161) (`94-agent-base-image-digest`) closes #94.
  - `agent/Dockerfile`: `FROM python:3.13-slim@sha256:7249d558…`, the tag's multi-platform index (covers amd64 and arm64).
  - `release.yml` build job: the new step *The base image's pin, against its tag* warns, never fails, when the tag has moved on from the pin. Its comment names the one `FROM` shape its `sed` reads. The publish jobs are unchanged.
  - `AGENTS.md`: Commands gets "The agent's base image": a one-line `&&` chain that reads the digest, checks its form and moves the pin. The subsection says the test holds the pin's shape, not that the digest is the index. The "One version" bullet names the base pin.
  - `.agents/releases.md`, checklist step 3: refresh the base in its own PR before the bump PR, whose Tests section records the base digest and that its Release run reported it current.
  - `.agents/releases.md`, *Re-run all jobs*: corrected; the base is now fixed and only timestamps differ (measured: file times and 296 `.pyc` files).
  - Brief §2.4 and spec §5.6: one sentence each.
- **Tests:** 26 new in `test_release.py`.
  - Parsing: the `FROM` parser splits at Docker's white space and lines only at newlines. The one-stage test holds the shape the step's `sed` reads, and a tab-separated second stage is counted and refused.
  - Workflow: the step runs under bash against a stand-in `docker` for three registry answers (current, moved on, unreadable). On the PR's own Release run on the runner it printed `python:3.13-slim still names sha256:7249…`, and both platforms built from the pin.
  - Documentation: the `AGENTS.md` line runs as written without `set -e`, for one good answer and six bad ones.
- **Decisions:** PR Deliberate calls 1–7. The pin is the index digest. The digest moves by hand at each release, not by Dependabot. The server's Dockerfile is not pinned: it is built where it runs and never published. A stale pin is a warning, not a failure. Rebuilding a tag to the same digest is out of scope. The outside read overruled none.
- **Review:** Greptile read `b6fbc68` (one P2, fixed in `df40a87`). In place of its re-run, an outside read by Codex (GPT-6.1 Sol) at `df40a87`: GO, one P3 (a tab-separated second `FROM` went uncounted) and three corrections, all fixed in `7d298f1` and read by the orchestrator against the remedy, with the reviewer's mutant replayed. Codex automatic review clean on every head.
- **State:** merged. No schema change; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` unchanged; no new agent import or listed name.
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR. At each release, checklist step 3's base refresh comes first.
- **Known gap, not this PR's:** a secret given to a `build` step through `env` passes every shape test in `test_release.py`. Added to #159's scope with the owner's go-ahead.

## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #153: no DNS reply over the advertised payload is parsed (PR #155)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `c128179`, at head `793eff3`; branch deleted. Entry committed by the orchestrator, with the review, CI and state lines brought up to date. `793eff3` is GitHub's merge of `main` (holding PR #158) into the worker's last head `9b2acdc`, with no hand-written change (its tree equals `git merge-tree` of the two); the orchestrator made it so CI would test the PR with the #156 fix, and the `agent` job passed there.
- **Done:** [PR #155](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/155) (`153-dns-reply-size`) closes #153.
  - `agent/src/tlg_ipam_agent/probe.py`:
    - `DNS_PAYLOAD = 1232` is both the advertised payload and the largest reply the agent reads.
    - `QuerySocket.datagram_received` answers an over-size datagram from the query's server and port with `ReplyTooLarge` (a `DNSException`): that attempt ends as no usable answer and the next server is asked.
    - An over-size datagram from any other source is dropped. `_from_server` matches dnspython's source rule or is stricter.
  - Spec §5.2 (the limit, what it refuses, the entry count and the estimate), brief §4.6 and `AGENTS.md` are updated.
  - Tests: 15 new in `test_probe.py`. Agent suite on `main` after this and PR #158: 1,405. Server suite 1,340, unchanged.
  - Review: GPT-6.1 Sol was stopped by its safety filter a few minutes into the briefed round and posted nothing (`.dev/153/manual-handover.md`); the round ran on GPT-6 Sol with Daybreak Blue: GO at `a06bd29` with one P3 (the comparison figure was called a ceiling on a false premise), fixed in `9b2acdc` with a numbered reply on the PR, and read by the orchestrator against the remedy. Before the round the orchestrator sent back one wrong figure (203 records; a root question is 5 bytes, so 244). Codex clean on `793eff3`; Greptile last read `051cd71`, clean.
- **Decisions:** PR body Deliberate calls 1–7. Size checked at arrival, in the agent's socket. An over-size reply ends the attempt only when it comes from the server. 1,232 kept: 710 bytes is the largest ordinary answer built, not the largest possible. No exhaustive bound on parse work is claimed.
- **Measured (M1 Ultra):**
  - At most 244 entries (5-byte root question); 29,646 comparisons is an estimate, 0.40 s here.
  - The worst reply built parses in about 0.116 s and holds the loop 115–119 ms; root entries do not raise that.
  - A 9,856-byte reply takes 8.7 s.
  - Two colliding answer records make 2, 4 or 6 comparisons, depending on the hash.
- **State:** merged. No schema change; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` 2; no new import or listed name. Its CI `agent` job failed three times before PR #158, only in `test_answers.py`'s memory test (#156, not this PR's).
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR. The record budget before parsing (Deliberate call 7) is filed as #160.
- **Untested:** an over-size reply over a real network path; the timings on CI hardware; the full suite on Python 3.11 (the reviewer ran the new cases on 3.11.18).
- **Next:** #160. #20 and #93 are still open in `probe.py`.

## 2026-11-25 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #156: the answer-cap memory tests measure the client in a fresh interpreter (PR #158)
- **Merged:** 2026-11-25, squash `606e1d4`, at head `34c9b40`; branch deleted. Entry committed by the orchestrator, with the review and state lines brought up to date.

  - Cause: each check-in makes httpcore try `import sniffio` (not installed). Under pytest the attempt passes the assertion-rewriting hook, whose `PurePath` interns a fresh string that is then freed (mortal in 3.13). That uses up a slot of the interned-string dict, and when the slots run out the dict rebuilds its table (3,844,800 B in a full run). `tracemalloc` counts the new table in full. The free-slot count at the test moves with every collected module's text: 49 on `main`, 20 on `main` + PR #155 in a `python:3.13-slim` container.
  - Reproduced in that container with the test unchanged. A plugin (`edge156.py`, in the PR body) uses up interned slots before the test, and the merged tree then fails with CI's figures: 4,022,997–4,028,543 and 5,314,720–5,315,023 B. After the fix, all 10 shifts pass. The orchestrator re-ran one shift of each: 5,313,160 B held before, passing after.
  - `agent/tests/answer_memory.py` (new, not collected) runs the client in `python -I`: no pytest, no hook, no server. It warms up with one check-in and measures the second. `test_answers._held` runs it, with kill and reap on timeout or cancel (Greptile P2, fixed in `34c9b40`). The compressed-answer, plain-answer and raw-chunk tests use it with their limits unchanged.
  - New `test_the_measurement_sees_a_client_that_holds_too_much`: a reader with twice the cap (2.7 MB) and one that decodes first (81 MB) are each over their limit.
  - Tests: agent 1,388 → 1,390. Container: 1,388 + 2 skipped on the branch, 1,403 + 2 skipped merged with PR #155.
  - Review: Codex clean on `34c9b40`; CI green (9 checks). Greptile read `0b0d71e` (one P2, fixed); in place of its re-run, an outside read by Codex (GPT-6.1 Sol) at `34c9b40`: GO, no findings (`.dev/156/review-1.md` and the PR). It read the interned dict's free slots directly (unchanged over 20 check-ins in the child) and ran the plain test 50 times: largest 1.84 MB, 1.87 MB under CPU load, against 2.10 MB.
- **Decisions:** PR body Deliberate calls 1–4. A child process, because `tracemalloc` has no filtered peak and the rebuild is on the client's own stack. Limits kept, with the budget and measured figures stated: plain 1.47 MB on Linux, 1.57–1.87 MB on macOS, against 2.10 MB; measured, not derived as a bound. The raw-chunk test moved too, for one rule.
- **State:** merged. No schema, protocol, brief or `AGENTS.md` change.
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR.
- **Untested:** the CI runner's exact interned-slot state; Python other than 3.13.
- **Next:** PR #155's `agent` job on a head that holds this fix.

No server change.
  - Review: Codex clean and Greptile 5/5 on `cbc4c9c`; CI green (10 checks). Briefed round by Codex (GPT-6.1 Sol) at `cbc4c9c`: GO, its round-3 finding on PR #130 closed, no new findings; it replayed the reproduction on a real systemd 257 and ran 30 probes of the ordering (review on the PR and in `.dev/138/review-1.md`).
- **Decisions:** Deliberate calls 1–6 in the PR body: the unit discards rather than the installer reading `systemctl show-environment`; `SSL_CERT_FILE` is not unset with `--ca-file`; `SSL_CERT_DIR` is unset even with it; bare names; an owner's drop-in is the owner's; only the two OpenSSL variables. No new import or listed name, so `test_invariants.py` is unchanged.
- **State:** merged. No schema change. A unit installed before this keeps its old text until `install` is run again.
- **Waiting on the owner:** nothing from this PR.
- **Untested:** an LXC, distributions other than Debian, systemd versions other than 257.
- **Next:** #136 and #140 each edit `install.py`, one at a time. The image `ipam130-sol-systemd:review` on this machine runs systemd for installer checks; `docker exec` in it starts with umask 0000, so set `umask 022` first or `trust.check` refuses the venv.

- **Done:** [PR #151](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/151) (`149-cname-walk-keys`, head `ad268e2`) closes #149. (Written by the orchestrator from the PR body: the worker's thread was still finishing when the PR merged.)
  - `probe.answered_name` keys its CNAME map and visited set by `Name.to_digestable()` (the name's wire form, labels lowercased, lengths included), not by `dns.name.Name`, whose hash in dnspython 2.9.2 ignores where labels split. A 512-link chain of colliding names went from about 724,000 `Name.fullcompare` calls to one. The walk visits the same names in the same order.
  - Spec §5.2, brief §4.6, `AGENTS.md` and `read_reply`'s docstring state the order the code already had: a PTR at the chain's end is the name (its first record when there are several), whatever else the reply holds; a PTR elsewhere is never the name, and with none at the chain's end makes the reply no usable answer.
  - Tests (`agent/tests/test_probe.py`, +5): a chain of colliding names costs work in step with its links, counted with `sys.setprofile` and never timed, asserting its own premise that the names hash alike; of several PTR records at the answered name the first is read (Greptile's P2); the counting helper puts back the profiler hook it found (the briefed round's P3).

- **Decisions:** PR body Deliberate calls 1–4: the key is `to_digestable()`; the test counts work; the spec says the first of several PTR records is read; the spec says the parse is dnspython's own cost.
- **State:** merged. No new import, query or wire change (`PROTOCOL_VERSION` 2). No schema change.
- **Waiting on the owner:** whether to file an issue for the remaining exposure the review describes: dnspython's parser has the same collision cost before the walk runs (1.67 s for a 65 KB reply, measured by the reviewer with a substituted socket), and the agent does not refuse a reply larger than the 1,232 bytes it advertises. Only a resolver the owner configured can send one.

## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #132: bound request heads, idle connections, pipelining and stalled readers below the app (PR #135)
- **Done:** [PR #135](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/135) (`132-header-idle-bounds`, head `e9855bb`) closes #132. (The worker's own last entry covered round 4 only; the orchestrator has put the whole PR here.)
  - The image's `CMD` is `python -m app.serve` (`api/app/serve.py`): uvicorn's own command line, so `UVICORN_*` settings still apply, with six pinned over it (`workers=1`, no reload, `http=header_limit.HeaderLimitProtocol`, no HTTP/2, `ws="none"`, 5 s keep-alive).
  - `api/app/header_limit.py`, a subclass of uvicorn's httptools protocol:
    - a head (request line and headers) of at most 64 KiB as received, `431 HEADERS_TOO_LARGE`, counted exactly by cutting reads where a head and a declared body end; a head after a chunked body in the same 4 KiB piece is the one stated exception;
    - 10 s for a connection to deliver a head, closed without an answer;
    - one pipelined request held ahead, with reading paused meanwhile;
    - 60 s for a client that stops reading (a paused send buffer, or a closed connection that does not flush), then abort;
    - a refusal by the parser follows one rule: uvicorn's `400` only when no earlier request is still unanswered; otherwise the refused request is dropped without running and the earlier answers go out in order, the connection closing after the last. Plain uvicorn, and `main` before this, wrote the `400` in front of them, which could hide a committed write;
    - a stated bound on parser calls a sender can force.
  - Docs: brief §2.2, §4.3 (the "below the app" paragraph and "Left to the reverse proxy or tunnel"), the `/mcp` refusal table and §9 row; spec §5.7 and §8 (the proxy checklist for nginx, Caddy and Cloudflare Tunnel, every line from documentation); `AGENTS.md` bullets "One uvicorn worker" and "One request-body cap"; README. Four decision comments on #132 record what the image bounds and what stays the proxy's.
  - Merged: `83e392e`, squash, on the owner's go-ahead; the remote branch is deleted. CI green and Codex clean on `e9855bb`; Greptile last read `e2c2ff3`, and the briefed rounds were the outside read after it. The briefed review took six sessions:
    - GPT-6.1 Sol at `e2c2ff3` was stopped by its provider's safety filter before a verdict (hand-over in `.dev/132/manual-handover.md`); its five leads went to the worker.
    - GPT-6 Sol with Daybreak Blue, started from T3 Code, then gave NO-GO at `9ec525c` (two P2s in §8's proxy advice), NO-GO at `48148de` (P2: a committed write lost its answer behind a parser refusal), NO-GO at `4df1508` (P2: the same by another route), and GO at `e9855bb` with no finding. The owner kept the Opus 5.5 worker after the second and third NO-GO.
    - Between the second and third, the orchestrator measured that a fix it had suggested (cutting chunked bodies at every CRLF CRLF) let a sender hold the worker 1.3 s per 1.9 MB body, and sent it back before the round; `.dev/132/crlf_cost.py` and `lost_answer.py` are the scripts.
    - The orchestrator ran 1,321 server tests on `e9855bb`. No follow-ups filed.
- **Decisions:** Deliberate calls in the PR body. A floor in the image plus the proxy in front; httptools kept, not h11; the limit is on bytes as received; 64 KiB although Cloudflare passes 128 KB; no concurrency limit in the image; the subclass relies on uvicorn 0.54 internals (`cycle`, `pipeline`, `flow`, the parser callbacks), held by the tests on both event loops.
- **State:** merged. Server 1,321 on its head (192 of them this PR's). No schema change.

## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #86: bound list_scan_findings' work per call and the size of one finding (PR #129)
- **Done:** [PR #129](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/129) (`86-bound-findings-call`, head `f77e53f`) closes #86.
  - `findings.list_named` reads each subnet in chunks (`_read_chunk`) along `(subnet_id, ip_int)` indexes on hosts, observations and dismissals.
    - At most `CALL_STATEMENTS` (100) statements per call, each returning at most `SCAN_CHUNK` (250) rows per table.
    - Inside a scanned DHCP pool, observations are read only at the chunk's hosts' addresses.
    - Subnets that cannot hold a finding cost no statement. A call that hits the limit returns a short or empty page with a cursor.
  - The scope read uses a covering index on reserved ranges `(subnet_id, scan_excluded, start_int, end_int)`. A subnet holds at most `schemas.SUBNET_RESERVED_RANGES_MAX` (100) reserved ranges on every add path, counted under the write lock.
  - A domain is at most 253 characters (`ipnet.DOMAIN_MAX`), so a finding is at most `FINDING_MAX` (5,000) bytes; the largest built is 4,880. A host whose domain was stored longer is listed with `host_fqdn` null.
  - The page budget counts brackets and commas. A cursor address above 64 bits never reaches SQL.
  - Startup creates declared indexes missing from existing tables (`database._add_missing_indexes`).
  - Docs:
    - brief: tool row, §3 domain and range rules, the `add_reserved_range` row;
    - spec: §5.8 and new §7.2 invariant 9;
    - `AGENTS.md`: the MCP bullet and the schema bullet;
    - the tool docstrings.

## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #90: a budget and a deadline on request bodies (PR #131)
- **Done:** [PR #131](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/131) (`90-bound-unauthenticated-bodies`, head `4efe69d`) closes #90.
  - `body_limit.py`: beside the cap, `BODIES_MAX` (16 MiB) bounds the body bytes of all requests in progress, read by the middleware or by the route, counted until the request ends (`503 UNAVAILABLE` past it). `BODY_TIMEOUT_S` (60 s from the headers, whole body) gives `408 REQUEST_TIMEOUT`.
  - Every refusal and every response sent before its body has fully arrived closes the connection. Without that, uvicorn keeps reading and discarding a trickled body after an early `401`.
  - A body the middleware reads is one buffer. A route that reads its own body gets blocks of at least 64 KiB.
  - Docs: brief §4.3 (limits, measurement, "Left to the reverse proxy or tunnel"), §5.1 table, acceptance list; spec §5.7 and the §6.6 row; the `AGENTS.md` cap bullet.
  - Merged: `c5d7139`, squash, on the owner's go-ahead; the remote branch is deleted. CI green and Codex clean on `4efe69d`. Greptile reviewed only the first head (`4598df7`, one P2, fixed and resolved) and was not retriggered. Briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol): GO with no findings at `4efe69d`; it repeated the memory measurement on Linux (about 63 to 65 MiB before, about 20 MiB after, both parsers) and ran the body-limit tests six times without a failure. The orchestrator ran 1,059 server tests on the head merged with `main` and repeated the real-server run: 9 held and answered `408` at 60 s, 23 refused. The header and idle-connection follow-up is proposed to the owner and not filed. Nothing else from this entry is waiting on the owner.
- **Decisions:** PR body Deliberate calls 1–11. Budget plus deadline, no spooling. The count runs until the request ends, not until it authenticates, because FastAPI parses a `Body` before the route's dependencies: check-in and the owner routes read a declared-length body before their credential, which the issue had missed. Bytes are counted as they arrive, never reserved. 60 s is twice the agent's 30 s request deadline. A cut body reaches the route as `http.disconnect`, and the middleware swallows the `ClientDisconnect` that follows.
- **State:** server 1058 and agent 922 pass; CI green on `4efe69d`; no schema change; `PROTOCOL_VERSION` unchanged.
  - Measured on macOS, loopback, 32 stalled 1.75 MiB bodies: `a8534eb` +60 to +68 MiB on both parsers; the new head +17 to +21 MiB, 9 held, 23 refused, `408` at 60 s.
  - Codex: one P1 on `4598df7` (per-chunk memory), fixed in `4efe69d`, then clean on `4efe69d` after `@codex review`. Greptile: one P2 on `4598df7` (same finding), fixed and resolved; it has not reviewed `4efe69d`.
  - Measured, not fixed (outside #90): uvicorn's httptools takes unbounded request headers (8 MiB of one unfinished header was accepted), and idle connections stay open on both parsers. Brief §4.3 lists both as the proxy's job.
- **Waiting on the owner:** the briefed round (an author from a different model family; the orchestrator briefs it), a Greptile retrigger on `4efe69d`, then merge. Whether to file a follow-up for header size and idle connections (uvicorn options in the image, or the proxy's job only).
- **Next:** #31 can take its proxy guidance from brief §4.3 and the PR body's "Left to the reverse proxy or tunnel". #86 may change the one brief §4.3 sentence that measures an import, which this PR left alone.

## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Sonnet 5.5), worker — #125: DNS hand-off guard test (PR #127)
- **Done:** [PR #127](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/127) (`125-dns-handoff-guard-test`, head `6559de8`) closes #125. Tests only, plus a comment.
  - `agent/tests/test_dns_send.py`: the DNS counterpart of `test_icmplib_send.py`. It runs the real `DnsResolver.ptr`, the installed dnspython and `QuerySocket` on the real `scan.Pacer`, with 12 concurrent lookups and an always-overdue clock. Only `create_datagram_endpoint` on the running loop is replaced. The stand-in transport records whether the slot of the lookup that sent the datagram is still open (`pacer.ended < granted[address]`), and answers through `datagram_received`. It sends no packet.
  - The control puts an `await` ahead of `dns.asyncquery.udp` and expects every query to reach the transport after its slot has ended.
  - `scan.py`: three comment lines in `Pacer`'s docstring (a `Pacer` belongs to one event loop). No other file changed.
  - Merged: `45712d5`, squash, on the owner's go-ahead; the remote branch is deleted. CI green, Codex clean and Greptile clean on `6559de8`, the only head. The orchestrator ran 924 agent tests on that head (Python 3.13) and repeated the mutation: the new test fails on the open-slot assertion and the control passes. The Python 3.11 count and the blind-check control are the worker's alone. No follow-up filed. Nothing from this entry is waiting on the owner any more.
- **Decisions:**
  - The open slot is judged per lookup, not by a pacer-wide count. A global count reported late sends as open when another lookup's slot was open.
  - The ARP request needs no counterpart: no library sits between its slot and `PacketLink.sendto`. Said in the PR.
- **State:** green. Agent suite: 924 passed on Python 3.13.12 and on 3.11.18 (922 on `main` at `a8534eb`, plus 2). CI is green on `6559de8`. Codex and Greptile both reviewed the head with no findings, and none are open. Mutation (a `sleep(0)` before `dns.asyncquery.udp`): the new test fails on the open-slot assertion and the control passes. Control with a blind check: the control fails. The mutation worktree was removed and never committed. No schema change.
- **Waiting on the owner:** the merge.
- **Next:** nothing from this entry. #22 owns the rest of `agent/`; the one new file in `agent/tests/` and the `Pacer` docstring edit should not conflict with it.

## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #21: enrolment tokens, the enrol exchange and re-enrol (PR #124)
- **Done:** [PR #124](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/124) (`21-enrolment-tokens`, head `b45a723`) closes #21.
  - "Add agent" creates the agent `pending` with a single-use `tlge_` token good for an hour.
  - `POST /api/v1/agent/enrol` (`services/enrolment`, on its own `enrol_router`, outside `require_agent`) exchanges the bearer for the agent token. One transaction, under the write lock, with the time read after the lock: a compare-and-set on `used_at`, the old agent token revoked, the new one issued, the agent `active`, the agent's own audit entries pruned to 100.
  - `POST /agents/{id}/re-enrol` issues a new enrolment token.
  - A global throttle sits in front: 10 failed attempts per 60 s, in memory.
  - The exchange is logged `source="agent"` with `actor`.
  - Re-enrol, update, revoke and delete read the agent under the write lock (`agents._locked_agent`).
  - Scope extension decided by the orchestrator: results ingest rechecks the agent's state and its token under the write lock (`results._credential_live`), which also closes the owner-revoke race that was on `main`.
  - Docs: brief (new §4.8, §4.6), spec §4.2/§5.4/§5.6/§5.7, `AGENTS.md`, README.

Greptile last reviewed `c3804f1` (three P1s, all fixed and answered) and needs a manual retrigger. The first briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol, on `c72e0a8`) was NO-GO with three P2s and a P3; all are fixed on `b45a723` (call 17). The branch is not merged with `main` at `c28abfb`. ## 2026-11-24 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5) — #121: packet slots measured from the hand-off; PR #123 open
- **Done:** PR #123 (head `2875149`), which closes #121.
  - On `main` the pacer measured each slot from the grant before it, so a hand-off delayed past a slot let the next packet go at once. New tests show it for all three kinds (gap 0 s). An in-process sweep with no packet sent showed it on `main` (0.045 ms apart in a Linux container); this head has no gap under 5.08 ms.
  - The fix, in `scan.py`: `Pacer` holds its turn until a `call_soon` callback after the step the slot was granted in, and measures the next slot from then. Each probe takes its own slot in `_Pool`. `PACE_YIELD_EVERY` is removed, since the loop now turns between any two grants.
  - Documents: spec §5.6, the README's `MAX_PPS` row, `agent/README.md`, `AGENTS.md` (the budget sentences only) and the brief §2.
  - Merged: `d067821`, squash, on the owner's go-ahead; the remote branch is deleted. CI green and Codex clean on `2875149`. Greptile reviewed only the first head (`01bcf3f`, the same P1 as Codex) and was not retriggered. Briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) at `2875149`: GO with one P3, filed as #125; it would overrule none of the seven deliberate calls, and measured a larger cost than the one below for a mixed job (about 5 to 6% at 200 pps, single runs). The orchestrator ran 921 agent tests on the head merged with `main`, on Python 3.13 and 3.11, and saw the 18 stall tests of the first head fail against `main`'s `scan.py`; it re-measured none of the timings. Nothing from this entry is waiting on the owner any more, and #120 is merged.
- **Decisions:** the owner's fork, option 1, with one rule for all three kinds. The PR's Deliberate calls give why it is a step-end callback rather than a stamp from each sender, and why the 64-send yield went.
- **State:** CI green on `2875149`. 920 agent tests (897 on `main`); server and web untouched; no schema change.
  - Codex: one P1 on `01bcf3f` (the pool hung when a lookup failure cancelled a task before its first step). Fixed in `2875149` with a test, both threads answered and resolved, and Codex clean on `2875149`.
  - Greptile reviewed `01bcf3f` (the same P1), so it reviews again after its cap; it needs a manual retrigger on `2875149`.
  - Cost of the fix: a 254-address sweep at 200 pps takes under 1% longer (4.41 s to 4.44 s for ICMP, 9.52 s to 9.59 s for ICMP and ARP, in a Linux container).
- **Waiting on the owner:** the briefed review round for #123 (a different model family; the orchestrator briefs it), the Greptile retrigger, and the merge. Older items as in the 2026-11-23 entry: #100, #91, #5, #25.

- **Done:** PR #122 (closes #120), head `848212f`. Tests only, one file: `agent/tests/test_probe.py::test_the_choice_of_servers_ends_with_the_jobs_deadline` freezes the event loop's clock (`frozen + skew`) before the scan is created, waits for the read thread with turns of the loop, moves the clock only once the read is held (+0.5 s: still running; +1.0 s: ended), and restores the real clock before its clean-up (the order of the #119 test). A 0.5 s sleep in the cancelled call (`scan.py`, applied then reverted) fails both cases; ignoring the job's deadline while the servers are chosen (`wait_for` removed, reverted) fails both. Agent suite 898 passed (897 plus the second case).

Not fixed, not filed: the late case waits for the read thread by spinning the event loop, so it keeps one core busy for 1.5 s; and the job's own clock is real, so the test still fails if the scan takes over a second of real time to reach the PTR stage, as it did before.

- **Done:** the real-network checks of #88, on a Linux host the owner gave access to, with the owner setting one host to drop ping and then powering it off. No file in the repository changed. The results are [a comment on #88](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/issues/88#issuecomment-7101806047); its boxes are ticked except the two hardware ones, and the issue is closed.
  - Set-up: the agent image and a throwaway server, both built from `main` at `ccd6ceb`, on the same host; the server behind a private CA, so no production data was involved. Nine sweeps (one of a routed /24, eight of the agent's own), each captured with `tcpdump`.
  - Passed: the sweep by the container with `CAP_NET_RAW`; PTR through the subnet's `dns_servers` and through the host's `resolv.conf`; TLS with a private CA, and a refusal without it or with the wrong host name; the agent suite on Python 3.11.18 (897 passed, the dev lock installed there); a host that drops ping found by ARP with its real MAC; a host that left not reported while the kernel still listed it `STALE` (its observation turned not alive, `last_seen_at` kept, its finding gone); the agent host's own MAC; a routed subnet given `icmp`, `rdns` and no ARP.
  - Not run: a parent interface with hardware VLAN offload or a bond (the host's interface is a container veth), and a segment with proxy ARP (none showed). "Complete" was read from the API; nobody looked at the Scanning section in a browser. A certificate from a public CA was not tried.
  - Rate at `MAX_PPS` 200: the agent's own packets never passed 195 in any second. The kernel's ARP during the ICMP sweep of the agent's own subnet (three requests for each silent address) reached 206 a second by itself and 273 with the agent's, the exception spec §5.6 names. On its own subnet the ICMP sweep put 20 to 22 echo requests on the wire, not 254: the kernel sends one only to an address it resolved.
  - Filed, on the owner's go-ahead: #121. In the routed sweep two echo requests left 0.15 ms apart, one of them about 5 ms late. The capture cannot say whether the delay was before the hand-off to the kernel (the agent's side, which the spec bounds) or after it (which it does not).
- **Decisions (the owner's):** an agent session ran #88, which the issue reserved for the owner. Results go on the public issue with no address, MAC or host name of the network. The test set-up was removed after the run. #88 is closed with the two hardware checks marked untested in its body: the only hardware of that kind carries production workloads, and the owner will not experiment on it.
- **State:** green and unchanged: `main` is `a8b58ec` plus hand-off commits (955 server, 897 agent and 118 web tests measured there; CI green). No PR is open and nothing is running. An agent built from `main` has now run on a real network. The test host holds nothing of the run but Docker build cache.

## 2026-11-21 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5), worker — #87: MAX_PPS on the wire (PR #110)
- **Done:** [PR #110](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/110) closes #87, head `7d8e91a`, cut from `main` at `9431428`. CI green; Codex clean on `7d8e91a`; Greptile not run (monthly cap); CodeRabbit skips the repo.
  - `scan.Pacer`: each grant at least `1/MAX_PPS` after the last, by the clock at the grant; no catching up after a late wake; re-sleeps after an early one; waiters served in turn (an `asyncio.Lock`); a cancelled waiter takes no slot. After `PACE_YIELD_EVERY` (64) grants in a row without a sleep, the next grant first yields.
  - The paced `_Pool` (ICMP) yields once after each start, so the echo request goes before the next grant.
  - `probe.QuerySocket`: the agent's own UDP socket, made before the slot and given to `dns.asyncquery.udp` as `sock`. The query is handed over in the grant's step, or dropped as an unsent attempt (`BlockingIOError`) if the socket can't take it; the socket is aborted when the attempt ends.
  - `loop.Agent` keeps one pacer for all jobs; `scan.run(pacer=None)` falls back to one per job.
  - Docs: spec §5.2 and §5.6, the `AGENTS.md` agent bullet (#107's file-keyword sentence untouched), both READMEs, the brief.
  - **Merged:** `d06018e`, by the orchestrator (Fable 5.2) on the owner's go-ahead; #87 closed. The round measured the cost of the strict bound, now in the PR body: 254 addresses at 50 and 200 pps took 18.1 s and 5.2 s against 15.3 s and 3.9 s on `main`, and a scan that completed inside a 5 s timeout on `main` ended incomplete here (names kept). Codex was clean on `7d8e91a`. The orchestrator re-measured: 650 agent tests on the head, and one real `icmp,arp,rdns` scan on a Docker `--internal` network with the image built from the head (complete; the ping-dropping host found by ARP; four PTR names through the new socket; queries no less than 0.204 s apart at 5 pps).
- **Decisions (PR body, calls 1–9):** strict bound on grants, no burst; item 4 closed (query dropped, not kept) and the budget carried across jobs; the kernel's ARP stays outside the budget by name, with numbers measured on a Docker `--internal` network (silent address: 3 requests 1 s apart; live peer: 1; `arp_accept 0`, so sweeping ARP first does not stop it). Yield bounds stated in sends: 64 for a ready task, fewer than 192 for one a timer wakes.
- **State:** agent 650 passed (macOS, and Linux in `python:3.13-slim`); server 808; web untouched. New `agent/tests/test_pacing.py` (19); +3 in `test_probe.py`, +1 in `test_loop.py`; `test_a_datagram_a_cancelled_lookup_had_handed_over_is_not_recalled` replaced; the failover test's `first-query-late` case tightened (late pacing sleep; grants and hand-offs asserted with no tolerance). - **Done:** [PR #102](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/102) closes #85, head `0c101f5`, cut from `main` at `7345d2a`. CI green on `0c101f5` (5/5); Codex clean on `0c101f5` (`has_any_findings: false`); Greptile not run (monthly cap); CodeRabbit skips the repo.
  - `hosts.update_host` compares the request's `last_seen` with the stored column as instants and skips the assignment when they are equal. A PUT that repeats the stored value, in any offset form, no longer moves `updated_at`.
  - `test_every_datetime_field_in_the_schemas_is_serialised_with_an_offset` now walks every model `schemas.py` defines except those in `REQUEST_MODELS`, and every model they reach (nested, list, dict, optional) wherever it is defined. Samples have the field's own shape; `AwareDatetime` and pydantic's other constrained datetimes count as datetimes.
  - `test_the_walk_finds_bare_datetimes_wherever_they_are_and_passes_annotated_ones` keeps the issue's three mutations in the suite.
  - The `AGENTS.md` datetime bullet and brief §3.4 (`updated_at`) and §4.3 are updated.

The orchestrator re-measured: 808 server tests on the head, and with the service comparison disabled the repeated-value test fails.
- **Decisions:**
  - The fix compares instants in the service; the validator still returns an aware value. Making offset-less input naive there would have left explicit-offset input moving `updated_at`.
  - An unchanged PUT still writes one `update` change entry with `before == after`, as on `main`. Only the row update and the `updated_at` move are gone.
  - The walk lists request models rather than response models, so a new model is checked as a response until it is listed.
  - The walk follows `agent_protocol` models that an owner response holds (`ScopeRangeOut` inherits `ScanRange`). Agent-route answers are never roots.
  - Fields typed `dict` or `Any` (audit snapshots) stay outside the walk; the snapshot test covers them.
- **State:** server suite 808 passed, agent suite 493 passed locally; web untouched. No schema change. Nothing half-finished.
- **Waiting on the owner:** merge of #102.
- **Next:** nothing left on #85. The `schemas.py` lane is free once #102 merges.

## 2026-11-20 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5) — #75: bound a subnet's dns_servers and what a job carries (PR #95)
- **Done:** [PR #95](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/95) closes #75, head `6352707`.
  - Job bound: `agent_protocol.DNS_SERVERS_MAX = 3`; `Job.dns_servers` has `max_length` set to it. `services/jobs` sends `subnet.dns_servers[:3]`, the first three in the order listed.
  - Subnet bound: `schemas.SUBNET_DNS_SERVERS_MAX = 8`, checked in `services/subnets._check_dns_servers` on create and on any update that sends `dns_servers`. Over it: `422` "dns_servers: a subnet may list at most 8 DNS servers, not N".
  - New `agent/tests/test_answer_limit.py` builds the largest check-in answer through the real route and the agent's real client. 104,978 bytes; 106,762 padded to the longest addresses; limit (`RESPONSE_MAX`) 1,048,576; the unbounded pre-#75 answer was 1,153,843. Shown red with the cut removed.
  - `test_contract.py` checks `probe.MAX_DNS_SERVERS == agent_protocol.DNS_SERVERS_MAX`.
  - Docs: brief §3.2, §4.5 (new paragraph "The answer fits the agent") and one acceptance line; spec §5.7; the `AGENTS.md` wire-contract bullet.
  - **Merged:** `e76471a`, by the orchestrator (Fable 5.2) on the owner's go-ahead; #75 closed. The owner kept eight as the subnet bound. An outside read (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) ran in place of Greptile after this entry was written: GO at `6352707` with one P3, filed as #97 (a subnet's CIDR is stored as typed, so a zero-padded prefix reaches the job; the "largest answer" test assumes the canonical spelling). Codex was clean on `6352707`. The orchestrator re-measured: 795 server and 465 agent tests on the head, and with the job's cut removed three tests go red (the answer-limit test on the model's own `max_length`, and on the size assertion only when that bound is removed too; the PR body now says so).
- **Decisions (deliberate calls on the PR, for the owner to overrule):**
  - Subnet bound 8, job bound 3: the field records what a subnet hands out; a job carries what the agent uses.
  - A subnet stored with a longer list keeps it. An update that leaves the list out or resends it unchanged is applied; changing it must meet the bound.
  - No `PROTOCOL_VERSION` raise: same shape, and the server now sends a subset of what it could before.
  - The agent does not start refusing a job with more than three servers.
- **State:** green.
  - CI passed on `6352707` (backend, agent, frontend, locks).
  - Codex completed on `6352707` with no findings; Greptile not run (monthly cap).
  - Server 795, agent 465 tests. Web and locks untouched. No schema change.
- **Waiting on the owner:** merge PR #95. Whether 8 stands as the subnet bound or becomes 3.
- **Next:**
  - #78 and #75 both edit `AGENTS.md` (different bullets) and the brief. The PR that merges second takes `main` in with a merge commit.
  - #20 must add its `fdns` names to the bound in `test_answer_limit.py`, which asserts `fdns == []` until then.
  - #68 and #85 follow in `schemas.py`.

## 2026-11-20 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5) — #14: active ARP sweep (PR #84)
- **Done:** [PR #84](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/84) closes #14, head `eeaafca`.
  - First head `347e64f` added the ARP sweep:
    - New `agent/src/tlg_ipam_agent/arp.py`: builds and reads frames, opens no socket.
    - `probe.PacketLink`: the `AF_PACKET` socket, protocol 0 until bound to ARP on one interface.
    - `probe.ArpSession`: takes a reply only from an address it asked; the first reply wins.
    - `scan.py`: one request per address, then one retry per still-silent address after 1 s. It runs after ICMP, on the same pacer and deadline.
    - `arp` is in `SUPPORTED`.
  - The briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) said NO-GO at `347e64f`: two P2s and two P3s. `eeaafca` answers it:
    - The listener yields after every 64 frames.
    - A send never waits for the socket to be writable.
    - A `loop.SCAN_GRACE_S` (30 s) backstop, and `_Pool` cancels its work in flight when cancelled.
    - A retry is skipped if its address is answered while the retry waits for its pacing slot.
    - Tests strengthened, each checked against a mutant (eleven in all).
    - Wording fixed in `AGENTS.md`, spec §5.2 and §5.6, the brief, the README and the PR body.
  - Docs, as of `eeaafca`: spec §5.2 (how the sweep runs; what an ARP reply cannot prove), §5.6 and §5.7, the brief, both READMEs, the `AGENTS.md` agent bullet.
  - Tests: agent 463 (374 on `main`); server 726 at `347e64f`, with no server file changed since.
  - Docker `--internal` runs only: a round trip, a capture of the requests, and a flood run. The scripts are in PR comments.
  - **Merged:** `7014b58`, by the orchestrator (Fable 5.2) on the owner's go-ahead; #14 closed. The second briefed round (Codex, GPT-6.1 Sol) ran after this entry was written: GO at `eeaafca` with two P3s (no yield in a late ARP send loop, filed in #87 with the `Pacer` finding and call 12; stale PR-body wording, corrected). The orchestrator re-measured: 463 agent tests, and the Docker `--internal` ARP round trip on `eeaafca`. Acceptance line 2 (a real VLAN) was not verified before the merge, by the owner's decision; it is in #88.

## 2026-11-19 — Claude Code (Opus 5.5) — #60: one request-body cap (PR #80)
- **Done:** [PR #80](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/80) closes #60, head `ee8ef97`.
  - New `api/app/body_limit.py`: `BODY_MAX` (2 MiB), the only definition, and `BodyLimitMiddleware`, added last in `main.py` so it wraps the whole app.
  - A declared `Content-Length` over the cap is `413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE`, refused unread. A declared length is trusted only on a request with no `Transfer-Encoding`; any other body is counted and refused at the chunk that crosses the cap. The refusal sends `Connection: close`.
  - It covers every route, `/mcp` included. `routers/agent.py` `_capped_json` and `agent_protocol.RESULTS_BODY_MAX` are removed.
  - Docs: brief §4.3, §4.6, §5.1, §7 and §10; spec §5.7 and §6.6; an `AGENTS.md` invariant line.
  - Tests: 726 server (687 + 39), 374 agent, 85 web; lint and build pass. No schema or lock change.
  - **Merged:** `2526705`, by the orchestrator (Fable 5.2) on the owner's go-ahead; #60 closed.     - Check-in loop with the spec §5.7 refusal rule (`agent_protocol.CHECKIN_RETRY_*`: 10–300 s jittered back-off; 13.5–15 min for refusals only the owner can clear). Only a 200 in the check-in shape queues a job.
    - Jobs one at a time. `guard.plan` refuses before any packet a job outside `ALLOWED_CIDRS`, with ranges outside its subnet, or over 4,096 addresses, and reports the refusal.
    - Paced ICMP sweep. PTR lookups sent query by query: one UDP query per server, at most 3 servers, EDNS(0)/1232, no retransmission, no TCP, truncated replies unused. Every datagram is paced by `MAX_PPS`; a lookup's 2 s start at its first slot.
    - 10-minute timeout gives `complete: false`; `arp`/`fdns` not run here also give `complete: false`.
    - HTTP: TLS always verified, https except to loopback; one 30 s deadline per request; results also capped by the lease; undecoded reads checked against 1 MiB before they are held.

  - `services/agents.authenticate` only reads. It leaves the accepted token's id in `session.info`.
  - New `agents.record_token_use`: a SQL-conditional `UPDATE` that runs inside the serving request's transaction. Check-in calls it after its claim; results ingest calls it after `_release`. A refused request never writes token use.

  - `jobs.checkin` and `results.ingest`: on `OperationalError`, roll back and answer 503 UNAVAILABLE via `errors.database_unavailable`. That logs the SQLite error name, never SQL, parameters or the bearer. The message says "busy" only for a lock.

    - the prune `entity_type` case;

Briefed round 1 (GPT-6.1 Sol) NO-GO at `cb83075`: P2 fixed and both review P3s handled in `c6d8acc` (the lock-during-ingest 500 → #59). Round 2 not started. The owner merges.
  - `services/results.py` (new): `POST /api/v1/agent/results`.
    - Refuses the whole set with `409 JOB_NOT_CURRENT` unless `job_id` is both the agent's and a target's `current_job_id`.
    - Clears both lease copies in one transaction, each a compare-and-set. `last_scan_at` = `job_issued_at`.
    - Rejects records one by one, judged by `job_methods` and by the scope read under the write lock.
    - Only a `complete` job issued a liveness method marks addresses dead. The 90-day prune covers every subnet.

  - `services/scans.py`:
    - `scan_scope` reads the subnet, the target's `scan_dhcp_pool` and the excluded ranges as committed columns, never from loaded objects and never refreshing them.
    - `drop_out_of_scope` takes the write lock (`take_write_lock`), decides membership in Python, and deletes by id in batches of 500.
    - Every scope-narrowing write calls `drop_out_of_scope`; subnet deletion deletes the subnet's observations.
  - Schema, additive: table `observations`; nullable `scan_targets.last_scan_error`, kept out of audit snapshots.
  - Docs:

    - Spec: §5.3, §5.4, §5.7.

  - `api/app/agent_protocol.py`: the wire contract (`PROTOCOL_VERSION = 1`; check-in request, interface, job and response shapes; caps on agent-reported fields, with `fullmatch` for capability tokens and MACs).
  - `services/jobs.py`: `checkin` runs the rate limit first (`_claim`: one per agent every 10 s, a check on the loaded row then a compare-and-set, `429` before any agent-row write), then the protocol check (`409`), then validation (`422`, recorded in `last_error` by field name only), then stores the fields and returns at most one job.   - `services/scans.request_scan`: 15-minute cooldown per subnet, as a compare-and-set.
  - Routes: `POST /api/v1/agent/checkin` (`require_agent` on the router, mounted outside the owner-key loop) and `POST /api/v1/subnets/{id}/scan` (`202`).
  - Schema, additive: `agents.current_job_id`, `agents.job_issued_at`, `scan_targets.job_methods`, all nullable.

## 2026-11-13 — Claude Code (Sonnet 5.5) — #4: scheduled backup pull and restore procedure (PR #42)
- **Done:** [PR #42](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/42) closes #4, head `21b7bba`. CI green. Codex: no findings (on `16303b1`). Greptile: three P2s, all fixed and answered; no re-review of `21b7bba` had arrived. CodeRabbit skips this repo (under 10 stars). Merge is the owner's. Docs and one `AGENTS.md` line only; no application code, endpoint or schema change.
  - `docs/backup-and-restore.md` (new):
    - **Pull:** a scheduled pull of `GET /api/v1/backup` from another host. A POSIX sh script writes dated `ipam-<UTC stamp>.db` files and keeps the newest N. Before saving or pruning it requires HTTP 200 (redirects not followed), a non-empty file, `integrity_check = ok` and the `sections`/`subnets`/`hosts` tables. Systemd timer and cron variants; cron mails stderr only.
    - **Restore:** `tlg-ipam-restore restore <snapshot>` and `undo <dir>`, one `set -eu` script. It refuses a bad snapshot before stopping the app, and checks the saved database exists before removing the current one.
  - README ("Backups and restore", "One worker"), the brief (§2.2 one-worker paragraph, §4.2 pointer, §9 row, two §10 lines) and an `AGENTS.md` invariant line for the single worker.
  - Measured on a local `docker compose` instance, from the doc's own code blocks. Pull, restore, undo and cleanup all worked. An empty 302, an empty 200, a foreign database and a wrong key were each rejected with no file left. A missing, empty and corrupt snapshot were each refused with the app still up. Output is in the PR body.
  - **Merged:** `59acad5`, by the orchestrator (Fable 5.2) on the owner's go-ahead; #4 closed. Before the merge, Codex and Greptile both re-reviewed `21b7bba` with no findings (Greptile 5/5), and the orchestrator re-ran pull, restore and undo at that head. Follow-up filed: #43 (pin `--workers 1` in the Dockerfile, M0). Live state after the merge: #35 and #36 are merged (entries below), so M0 has only #43 and #5 (`owner`, now unblocked) left. #6 was started next, in its own thread, with Opus 5.5.
- **Decisions:**
  - The restore moves the old `ipam.db`, `-wal` and `-shm` into `/data/before-restore-<stamp>/` instead of overwriting. After a normal stop the data was in a 168 KB `-wal` beside a 4 KB `ipam.db`.
  - Retention keeps the newest N files, not an age window, and runs only after a successful pull.
  - The integrity check opens the snapshot with `?immutable=1`. The snapshot is in WAL mode, and a plain open left `-wal`/`-shm` litter.
  - The one-worker reasons are the ones true on `main`: stateful MCP sessions (from the code, not reproduced), SQLite's single writer, and uncoordinated per-process startup (measured: two workers on a fresh database sometimes fail with `database is locked`). The spec's M3 linking-state reason is not stated in the brief or README, which describe `main`.
  - Not done: `--workers 1` in the Dockerfile `CMD`. `WEB_CONCURRENCY` would override the default, which is why the docs name it.
  - Orchestrator, with the owner (2026-11-13): the automatic reviews do not re-run after a fix commit. `.agents/orchestration.md` → "Verifying a result" now says how to retrigger them.
- **State:** backend suite 80 passed; frontend lint, 3 tests and build pass. Not run: the systemd timer, the cron line including the mail itself, a pull from another host over a real network.
- **Waiting on the owner:** merge PR #42. #5 (redeploy and a deliberate restore on the deployed instance) is the owner's; its restore step is `docs/backup-and-restore.md`. Decide whether to pin `--workers 1` in the Dockerfile.
- **Next:** #35 and #36 are independent of this. M1 follows: #6, #7, #8; #6 and #8 owe a briefed review round. #6 will edit the MCP auth parts of the README and the brief, which this PR left alone.

## 2026-11-13 — Claude Code (Sonnet 5.5) — #3: dependencies locked, FastMCP 4 pinned (PR #34)

- **Done:** [PR #34](https://github.com/DeusMaximus/TLG-IPAM/pull/34) closes #3, head `85e96f8`. CI green. Codex: no findings. Greptile: 5/5, no findings. Merge is the owner's.
  - `api/requirements.in` / `requirements-dev.in` hold the direct dependencies (`fastmcp>=4.1,<5`). `requirements.txt` / `requirements-dev.txt` are now generated locks: `uv pip compile --universal --python-version 3.12 --generate-hashes`, 71 and 78 pins. They resolve FastMCP 4.1.8, MCP SDK 2.3.0, FastAPI 0.162.5. The dev lock is constrained to the runtime lock.
  - The Dockerfile and CI install with `pip install --require-hashes`. `api/tests/test_mcp.py` reads `input_schema`, so the FastMCP deprecation warning is gone.
  - `AGENTS.md` has the new setup command and a "Dependency lock" section with the regenerate commands. README and the brief are updated.
- **Decisions:**
  - uv, not pip-compile, because `--universal` gives one lock for the 3.12 image, 3.13 CI, and any dev machine. Only `pip` is needed to install it; `uv` is needed only to regenerate.
  - The lock file names are unchanged and `--no-header` is used, so the locks hold no local paths. Recompiling reproduces them byte for byte.
- **State:**
  - Backend suite: 80 passed on Python 3.12 and 3.13 from fresh venvs. Frontend lint, tests and build pass. No schema change.
  - The image built on `python:3.12-slim` matches the lock. The container started, `/health` returned 200, and `/mcp` returned 401 without a key.
  - pytest-asyncio 1.5.0 prints a `PytestDeprecationWarning` at configure time about `asyncio_default_fixture_loop_scope` (unset). It is not a FastMCP warning, and `pytest.ini` is untouched. It is worth its own small issue.
  - There is no CI check that the locks match the `.in` files. Install-time `--require-hashes` catches a hand-edited lock.
  - Not tried: installs on Python 3.14 / 3.15.
- **Waiting on the owner:** merge PR #34. Reinstall the long-lived venv with the new setup command; it is still on FastMCP 3.4.2.
- **Next:** #4, then M1 (#6, #7, #8). From now on, dependency changes go through the `.in` files and a regenerated lock, never a hand-edited lock.

## 2026-11-13 — Claude Code (Fable 5.2) — v0.2 spec accepted; process skeleton added

- **Done:**
  - `docs/v0.2-spec.md`: the v0.2 spec (scan agents, remote MCP), accepted at draft 5 after a review against the code. It is the contract for unshipped work; the brief stays the description of `main`.
  - Process skeleton: this file, the Workflow section in `AGENTS.md`, `.agents/README.md`, `.agents/review-brief.md`, `.dev/` gitignored.
  - Brief: a real domain removed from the footer.
- **Decisions** (all recorded in the spec's §9):
  - Linking a remote MCP client uses a one-time code from the private UI. There is no `OWNER_PASSWORD`.
  - ARP scanning is an active request/reply sweep. The kernel neighbour table is not read.
  - Scan scope: the DHCP pool is not scanned unless enabled per subnet; reserved ranges are scanned unless excluded per range; exclusion is absolute and not configurable over MCP.
  - M2 is split: M2a (ingest, observations, scope, findings, container agent with a hand-made token) and M2b (enrolment, guided install, systemd installer).
  - Process: one issue, one branch, one PR; merge needs green CI and every automatic review finding (Codex, Greptile) answered; PRs touching authentication, tokens, OAuth, agent ingest, or packet-sending code also need a briefed round with a GO; the owner merges.
  - PR #1's automatic reviews found three gaps in the spec, fixed in the same PR: forward-DNS results had no storage, the systemd agent could not read its own token file, and a job with no liveness method could mark every address dead.
- **State:**
  - No application code has changed. Backend suite: 80 passed on the local venv (FastMCP 3.4.2), and 80 passed on a fresh install (FastMCP 4.1.8, MCP SDK 2.3.0) with one deprecation warning in `tests/test_mcp.py`.
  - `api/requirements.txt` is still unpinned, so a fresh `docker build` installs FastMCP 4.x today. Pinning is M0.
  - `api/tests/conftest.py` points every pytest session at one fixed temp database. Two sessions on one machine at the same time corrupt each other. Run one at a time until the M0 issue that gives each session its own database lands; it is the first thing to do before agents work in parallel.
  - Milestones and issues are not filed yet.
- **Waiting on the owner:** approval of the milestone and issue list; later, the M0 redeploy and deliberate restore test, the M3 client-compatibility spike (needs real web-client accounts and a public URL), and installing agents on the VLANs.
- **Next:** file milestones M0, M1, M2a, M2b, M3 and their issues once approved. Then M0 and M1 serially (they chain through `auth.py`, `main.py`, `mcp_server.py`, `models.py`). After M1, M2a and M3 can run side by side, and inside M2a the `agent/` package can be built against the §5.7 protocol in parallel with server ingest.
